## Boot and restart a guest with the current host trust roots

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Current locally confined Firecracker boot, host-only vsock delivery, installed public trust and readiness ordering.

Required refusal: Reject a wrong-boot trust exchange without changing installed trust, service availability or durable receipt.

Tested scope: Strictly typed bounded NixOS/Ubuntu native fixture on two colocated Lilly nodes; exact host roots, actual guest services, local HTTP and retained data on restart. Wrong, forged and expired delivery mutations each terminate in completed peer-EOF refusals before unchanged receipt, installed bundle and service checks and a final accepted same-boot delivery. Sequential three-NixOS admission checks usable startup and durable ownership. A valid 17 GiB-root request exceeds the independently read actual 16 GiB quota; the fixture does not impose a settled virtual-promise cap or rerun the concurrent capacity race.

- **property, recorded**: [a boot must pass pending and delivering before installed](repo:lilly/test/lifecycle/guest_trust_test.exs) — Generated retry transitions against the real Ash journal; no kernel/vsock claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-26-guest-trust-evidence.md).
- **property, recorded**: [digest is invariant under CA ordering and changes when a CA changes](repo:lilly/test/lifecycle/guest_trust_test.exs) — Generated independent root byte sets; digest behavior only.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-26-guest-trust-evidence.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [NixOS and Ubuntu boot trust survives refused stale delivery and renews on restart](repo:nix/tests/machines/fleet-guest-trust/driver.py) — Captured n99x4 on 0cfa6302: real packaged topology, both guest distributions, boot receipts, completed wrong-boot refusal by receiver EOF without response bytes, exact installed trust before repairing delivery, certificate-gated SSH readiness, service order, local HTTP and retained-data restart. Timeout fails the probe. Complete native candidate passed; later gateway startup-retry integration retains separate upstream evidence.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-fleet-guest-trust-verification.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [every wrong, forged and expired boot trust mutation ends in a peer-EOF refusal with no response bytes](repo:nix/tests/machines/fleet-guest-trust/guest.exs) — Recorded on 40c1e73b (drv f62qs50m): six probe executions across both distributions; per probe, one wrong-boot exchange plus seven mutations of exactly one receiver check (protocol version, placement, stale execution epoch, nonce, forged digest claim, duplicated root, private-key-bearing PEM), with unchanged receipt, installed bundle and services verified after each sweep and a final accepted same-boot delivery. One l1 stop escalated final-sigterm timeout to SIGKILL before stopping; recorded, unrelated to trust.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-26-guest-trust-evidence.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [real enlarged fixture pool admits three trust-enabled NixOS guests](repo:nix/tests/machines/fleet-guest-trust/driver.py) — Three usable NixOS boots and exact 15 GiB disk ownership on one actual pool; an otherwise valid root larger than its independently read quota must refuse without a new VM or changed ownership. This is sequential physical admission evidence, not a limit on settled virtual promises.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-fleet-guest-trust-verification.md).
- **property, present**: [a boot must pass pending and delivering before installed](repo:lilly/test/lifecycle/guest_trust_test.exs) — Generated retry transitions against the real Ash journal; no kernel/vsock claim..

**Gaps:** Native n99x4 passed both distributions with ADR-0124 certificate-gated readiness and the corrected physical refusal on 0cfa6302. Subsequent aaba77ee gateway startup-retry integration has separate upstream native evidence; this result remains attached to its captured base. Four rejected candidates and an orchestration-aborted attempt retain their exact scopes. Lifecycle/digest property executions and receiver-side failure-exhaustion are recorded on 40c1e73b in the 043 review (2 properties on seeds 0/43043; six probe executions, eight completed refusals each). ADR-0113's sender-side terminal trust-retry exhaustion (at most three attempts, then fencing; canonical 053), independent physical hosts and the seven legacy feature matrices remain separate. Historical native relocation evidence retains its captured revision.

**Preferred test work:** Rerun the seven legacy feature matrices on current source, verify sender-side terminal trust-retry exhaustion (053) and extend to independent physical hosts.

## Register a Git mirror, read published files, refresh through failure and remove it

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, recovery.

Boundary: Current administrator and passkey elevation share the audited catalog transaction; stored browser login/current registration gate reads; locked current attempt gates publication.

Required refusal: Ordinary users cannot mutate, demoted actors cannot admit late writes, removed/stale repository handles cannot read, and superseded or duplicate attempts cannot publish outcomes.

Tested scope: Actual public Catalog and Worker generated histories plus six deterministic three-independent-BEAM PostgreSQL schedules. Captured f4de549a-based tree 89e03703 passed the complete ordinary browser journey using packaged Marshall/Enrico, verified local HTTPS Git and virtual WebAuthn. The packaged Enrico service journey is recorded on current source with token/label refusals, idempotent attempt replay, force-push retention, outage preservation and recovery.

- **property, recorded**: [refresh failure and removal histories retain only published authority](repo:marshall/test/marshall/enrico/history_test.exs) — Eighteen bounded generated actual Catalog/Worker histories; independently retained previous head, exact audit actor/attempts, queue identity and stale-handle refusal after re-registration.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-git-mirror-verification.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [enrico-admin-transaction-ordering](repo:marshall/test/marshall/enrico/history_distributed_test.exs) — Six real-action PostgreSQL schedules across three independent BEAMs: demotion both orders, removal before success, replaced success/failure and duplicate outcomes. Synthetic verified HTTPS attempt service; no native fetch claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-git-mirror-verification.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [force-push keeps the previously published commit after Git pruning](repo:nix/tests/machines/enrico-service/driver.py) — Recorded on current source c14ae27d: packaged service with isolated verified HTTPS Git; token and invalid-label refusal without mirror state, clone/read, update, idempotent attempt replay, force-push retaining old refs after cache pruning, source-outage retention and recovery after the source returns. One QEMU VM; no ZFS quota or physical tunnel claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-26-git-mirror-refresh.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [git-browser-current-authority](repo:nix/tests/machines/git-mirrors/browser.py) — Captured tree 89e03703 on f4de549a: packaged Marshall/Enrico, real pre-elevation submission refusal without inventory change, virtual passkey elevation, ordinary tree/blob/history/raw reads, refresh/source outage retaining the published SHA, anonymous/stale-page/raw refusal, audit attribution and actual filesystem cache deletion. No ZFS quota or physical tunnel claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-git-mirror-verification.md).
- **machine, present**: [force-push keeps the previously published commit after Git pruning](repo:nix/tests/machines/enrico-service/driver.py) — Existing packaged service with isolated HTTPS Git, updates, retained old refs, token refusal and source outage; existing ADR record is retained without a new run..

**Gaps:** Independent physical tunnel, ZFS quota and 100 GiB cache pressure, large repository durations, physical authenticators and production rollout remain separate. The browser cache uses an ordinary VM filesystem. Generated service responses do not establish native Git/network behavior. The initial failed UI fixture run remains separately recorded.

**Preferred test work:** Retain generated action histories and recorded browser/service scopes; exercise physical tunnel and cache pressure in dedicated acceptance environments.

## Preview, request, pause and resume a host evacuation with current administrator authority

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Current administrator role, stored browser login and recent passkey elevation govern user controls through commit. Accepted fixed-cohort work has separate durable campaign authority.

Required refusal: Refuse stale elevation/login, ordinary users, stale workload revisions and readiness without fresh physical emptiness; pause admits no new move.

Tested scope: Recorded generated control and physical-report histories, three independent Marshall processes on one shared database, and a passing native browser/two-Lilly campaign on captured384b0fa9: ordinary preview/refusal, real elevated controls, running/stopped moves, physical cleanup, data and ingress preservation, fresh-elevation resume.

- **property, recorded**: [campaign histories preserve fixed authority and per-revision downtime consent](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/evacuation_history_test.exs) — Real public controls, independent consent/revision model, preserved admitted authority after demotion and complete refusal row equality.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-maintenance-evacuation-verification.md).
- **property, recorded**: [ready and resume require all current physical emptiness evidence](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/evacuation_history_test.exs) — Synthetic host/pool freshness and allocation histories before and after readiness; no native physical claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-maintenance-evacuation-verification.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [evacuation approval retains VM-before-User order with personal identity allocation](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/evacuation_distributed_test.exs) — Three independent BEAMs, real User/VM locks, both revocation orders, scoped complete inventories and failure cleanup; one shared database.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-maintenance-evacuation-verification.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [evacuation-real-admin-browser](repo:nix/tests/machines/maintenance-evacuation/browser.py) — Actual browser passkey enrollment/elevation, ordinary preview/forged refusal, request/pause/continue and fresh-elevation resume; virtual WebAuthn and one outer host.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-maintenance-evacuation-verification.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [continue moves running and stopped guests and readiness follows physical source cleanup](repo:nix/tests/machines/maintenance-evacuation/driver.py) — Source-built Lilly/ZFS/Firecracker moves, source dataset/unit/hold/slot/allocation cleanup, exact machine/address/ingress identity, real destination data and public HTTPS; stopped guest later starts with retained data.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-25-maintenance-evacuation-verification.md).

**Gaps:** Native running-workload downtime consent (every running move now needs it per ADR-0155), cancellation/replan and interruption permutations, physically independent hosts, hardware passkeys, Ubuntu evacuation and production rollout remain unverified.

**Preferred test work:** Extend the recorded bounded native campaign to running-workload downtime consent and cancellation/replan histories and Ubuntu; retain earlier relocation failure evidence with its original scope.

## Historical pre-cutover: Read fleet diagnostic logs in the Control room with scoped query authority

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, storage.

Boundary: Historical boundary at the captured pre-cutover revision; this reader or ingestion path is retired. Reads pass Cedar admin_read per call and the reader credential reaches only /select/* on the fleet-network entry; the store is a terminal destination that never authorizes domain actions and never receives audit, Firecracker or guest console classes.

Required refusal: Anonymous browsers redirect to sign-in; unknown viewer events halt; insert credentials are refused on /select/*; reader credentials are refused on /insert/*; unverified CA chains and plain unauthenticated requests fail.

Tested scope: NixOS machine test: real journald event delivered through collector, verified-TLS authenticated entry, right/wrong-credential refusals, two-source filtering and queued backfill after an entry outage, plus descriptor credential hygiene. Property tests pin LogsQL escaping and fail-closed filters; application tests pin page rendering, redaction narrative, tail and halted events.

- **manual, present**: [Retired evidence fleet-log-viewer item 1](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-09-observability-decoupling.md) — Reviewed historical scope of the retired source at the captured baseline; no current runtime acceptance claim..
- **manual, present**: [Retired evidence fleet-log-viewer item 2](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-09-observability-decoupling.md) — Reviewed historical scope of the retired source at the captured baseline; no current runtime acceptance claim..
- **machine, recorded**: [logstore-machine-verified-delivery](repo:nix/tests/machines/log-store/default.nix) — Two NixOS machines: durable journal capture, trusted normalization, verified TLS, path-scoped entry authority, right/wrong credential outcomes and outage backfill.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-26-fleet-log-viewer.md).

**Gaps:** A native browser Control-room journey over the real Marshall release, log-store CA rotation, production roll-out acceptance and multi-host fleet query behavior remain unverified; duplicate absorption after real queue backfill is covered by unit-level identity dedup only. ADR-0149 retires diagnostic ingestion/outbox/presentation; pre-cutover evidence is historical. Replacement native/properties must be executed separately.

**Preferred test work:** Execute the replacement independent-observability native and property scenarios; historical test receipts cannot establish the cutover. Current journeys are recorded in the independent-observability scenarios.

## Enroll a replacement host with fresh physical identity after retiring the old one

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Deployment enrollment binds each host to its real machine identity, name and ZFS GUIDs. Retirement is the only way to release host authority permanently; the retired row is never recycled or renamed.

Required refusal: Refuse enrollment for recycled retired names, conflicting registrations and unready or unfresh hosts; refuse stale compute and inference credentials over every authenticated transport after retirement; refuse all issuance, sessions, exchanges, enablement and admission on a retired host forever.

Tested scope: Generated histories over the real Ash actions and authenticated control exchange against a pure model oracle, three-BEAM races for name and rotation serialization, and a native two-Lilly fleet journey with SOPS delivery, real v3 TLS sessions, real ZFS GUID binding and a nested guest reaching private services. Physical providers, production DNS/CA/load and multi-region are excluded.

- **property, recorded**: [generated histories converge with the oracle and end retired](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/enrollment_history_test.exs) — Real operator file boundary, control session and report ingests, both admission entry points, and drain/resume/retire against a pure model with complete-table refusal audits; no native claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-27-host-replacement.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [a resumed drain remains in history after a new drain request](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/enrollment_history_test.exs) — Real SQL-backed host history retains the closed drain alongside the current open request; focused file also reruns the two generated properties at the previously failing seed. No physical host replacement is exercised.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-27-host-replacement.md).
- **property, recorded**: [credential histories preserve live guest reservations byte for byte](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/enrollment_history_test.exs) — Rotation, revocation, admission toggles and maintenance on a host holding a real reservation; retirement refused while resources are held.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-27-host-replacement.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [retirement fences concurrent rotations into one terminal retired state](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/distributed_enrollment_test.exs) — Three independent BEAMs over one committed database: same-name convergence/conflict, host-lock rotation serialization with idempotent prepared retries, and retirement fencing rotations in either lock order; seeded lock/fence mutants fire elsewhere in the suite inventory.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-27-host-replacement.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [the replacement prepares over the protected SSH bridge with fresh identity](repo:nix/tests/machines/fleet/scenarios/host-replacement.py) — Real two-Lilly fleet: SOPS delivery, orthogonal storage GUIDs, v3 TLS session, readiness-gated enable and nested guest private-service reachability before and after replacement; stale compute/Ziggy refusal over the real TLS listener.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-27-host-replacement.md).

**Gaps:** Physical bare-metal providers, production DNS/CA and load, multi-region and database-loss rebind remain unverified; the replacement journey reuses the deployed fleet's shared fixture PKI and QEMU identity model, and lost-response retries are covered by the generated/local evidence rather than injected network partitions.

**Preferred test work:** Retain the native two-Lilly replacement journey with fresh physical GUIDs and stale-credential refusal. Rebinding a surviving fleet onto a replaced control-plane database belongs to the historical-authority improvement.

## Open a public provider link, sign in, or run overalls open to create an OSS machine with a guest-owned checkout

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Public CSRF capture grants no authority and stores only canonical source2, visit and expiry in the signed cookie. A current ordinary stored login or registered-key Porsche identity admits through Compute and a matching manifest4 source capability; ordinary browser provisioning requires no passkey. The postcommit receipt rechecks login and VM ownership before matched clear. Lilly carries inert source2 bound to current placement, epoch and boot; only the guest contacts providers and runs git. Existing immutable snapshots/receiver records are never rewritten during cutover. Every validated new provision/start snapshot derives source from the durable VM, including supplied resize snapshots. Recovery preflights durable image eligibility without discarding valid crash/resize proof or fresh host reports.

Required refusal: Source queries/fragments never enter capture storage/POST; URL user information refuses there, and raw authority/path stays strict backend input. Malformed or expired capture, missing CSRF, revoked or MFA-pending identity creates no machine. Unsupported origins/routes, option-shaped values, non-egress profiles, absent/obsolete/mismatched source capability and changed source under one idempotency key refuse before allocation. A stale completion cannot clear a newer observed capture. Stale boots, source conflicts, foreign API clone origins, redirects and obsolete stored sources refuse; a done marker cannot bypass stored-source validation. Supplied snapshots cannot replace the retained immutable image or recorded source. Unavailable restart capability releases only confirmed stopped compute and retains disk/address; confirmed resize completes a fixed source-unavailable failure, preserving target sizes and ordinary cleanup.

Tested scope: Incoming browser-feature source2 grammar/manifest properties, SQL-backed capture/MFA/dedupe/admission histories, initial Lilly driver refusal, boot delivery/journal properties and Rust receiver/CLI tests retain their captured scope before ADR0158 integration. Frozen-fixture NixOS passed all twelve subtests, wrapper exit0 in1352s and script854.61s: cross-tab magic-link/required MFA, all twenty provider/revision exact guest HEAD/shallow/done assertions, installed-CA API/fork probes, retained restart, public immutable downloads and packaged CLI/workdir. Normal fragment capture composes through the final privacy-fixed frontend on local provider DNS/TLS/Git/API fixtures. Earlier corrected Ubuntu passed four initial GitHub and ten additional cases before Codeberg change stalled; latest frozen Ubuntu passed sign-in/MFA/dedupe in23.04s then failed initial guest startup before checkout. Complete Ubuntu and latest combined source2/generated-identity component/native acceptance remain separate. Earlier frontend and e2384877 source1 results retain historical scope.

- **machine, recorded**: [overalls open creates a sourced machine, waits for the guest clone and runs in it](repo:nix/tests/machines/repository-launch/driver.py) — Final NixOS actual packaged overalls CLI authenticates with registered key through Porsche/Audi, creates source2 VM, waits for guest checkout, runs the fixed command in expected working directory and refuses an invalid source without creation; subtest 32.75s. Local provider/fleet, no live provider or production activation.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-10-browser-launcher.md).

**Gaps:** Historical initial NixOS/Ubuntu captures failed; an Ubuntu diagnostic reached waiting before the source-handoff repair, and the corrected 1703s Ubuntu run stalled at Codeberg change after four initial and ten additional cases. Focused exact dnsmasq 2.93/musl evidence isolated fixture AAAA REFUSED; authoritative local-domain NODATA repaired the namespace probe and complete NixOS run. Latest frozen Ubuntu exited 1 after 266.734s at initial guest startup with Lilly execution_failed, after browser sign-in/MFA/dedupe passed; no checkout/restart/matrix/download/CLI was reached and startup cause remains under diagnosis. Complete current Ubuntu acceptance is unresolved. Live provider API/smart-HTTP, Firefox public-provider network, production cutover/download/checkout and Brady interception remain unverified. Private repositories/self-hosted origins are out of scope. Guest writers share the checkout by design. The signed pending cookie holds one intent; concurrent response ordering is not a multi-tab queue. Obsolete sourced machines require recreation; retained bundle custody has no source-v1 adapter. Final extension sentinel removal passed actual Chromium/Firefox actions; four pure capture minimizer tests/types are enforced by workspace.check. Normal fragment/cookie/form resume composes in current native browsers, but focused browser assertions of query/hash sentinel removal, capture storage contents and sanitized POST contents remain unrecorded.

**Preferred test work:** Complete the current Ubuntu family through cross-tab sign-in/MFA, all twenty provider/revision fixtures, retained restart and CLI; then add Brady-intercepted/live-provider and capture sentinel/storage/POST acceptance without promoting old source1 results. Test concurrent pending-cookie response ordering before promising multi-tab serialization.

## Find the browser extension guide, download an unsigned launcher and explicitly configure its server before a provider click

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Public and signed-in navigation and Overview link to the canonical public /docs/browser-extension guide. Guidance grants no identity or compute authority. Marshall serves a closed artifact allowlist from a canonical immutable Nix-store package over its existing public HTTPS boundary without login state; operators may disable downloads while the guide remains public. Strict native modules transmit only provider origin/path after removing source queries/fragments, only after an explicit provider toolbar click to an explicitly configured origin. Public package possession grants no compute authority; the repository-launch scenario owns authenticated capture and admission.

Required refusal: Disabled downloads, mutable/traversing roots, symlink root/bin/file, malformed descriptors, length mismatch, missing files and unknown artifacts return404. Missing or malformed server settings open options without transmitting. Known unsupported URLs disable the action. Withheld URLs preserve the explicit activeTab grant without transmitting; clicks validate the observed provider URL before opening Overalls. Source query/fragment fields are removed before transmission and URL user information refuses. Remote HTTP and credential-bearing server URLs refuse. Unsigned developer archives do not imply permanent Firefox installation.

Tested scope: Present controller/LiveView assertions inspect the canonical guide's two downloads, unsigned Chrome and temporary Firefox setup wording, public navigation and signed-in Overview CTA; no new real-browser discovery/setup execution is recorded. Recorded final strict typing/10 Node tests, deterministic minimized immutable package/check, real Chromium and exact immutable Firefox four-provider sentinel action clicks producing only origin/path launch URLs, unknown-URL click refusal, settings/metadata, developer/store byte equality and host immutable-download refusals. Final NixOS verified-TLS public artifact/manifest/checksum/no-cookie/unknown-selector assertions passed in 0.31s. Firefox uses fixed local TLS/certificate bypass; installed-extension auth/guest composition and production downloads remain separate.

Phoenix controller connections, no browser installation or deployed TLS claim..

## Submit bounded OpenTelemetry diagnostics from an eligible guest through Audi

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, transport, isolation, recovery.

Boundary: Audi gets a current Lilly placement binding and forwards an exact private OTLP route to the Rust receiver; only host-bound sanitized guest metrics reach the local collector.

Live rebinding, guest trace delivery across WireGuard and production guest data remain unverified.

## Historical pre-cutover: Deliver physical-host monitoring samples and recover after a conflicting ID

## Inspect source-owned candidate ratios in the private Grafana dashboard

## Read a guarded candidate for Underclass local monitor availability on both hosts

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, transport, isolation, recovery.

Boundary: A host-owned Rust probe calls only the pinned Underclass tokenless local Unix monitor; a separate collector intake assigns fixed Underclass-monitor and host provenance before Prometheus and private Grafana read it.

Required refusal: Malformed, stale, oversized or failed monitor responses produce an observed zero. A missing host, incomplete scrape window, stale probe, or unavailable collector makes the candidate unknown instead of healthy; other application UIDs cannot write the Underclass collector socket.

Tested scope: Rust properties cover response freshness and bounded fixed telemetry. Promtool checks observed zero, missing host, incomplete scrape coverage and retained-recording refusal. A two-node NixOS fixture runs the pinned Underclass service and probe through the real collector, including monitor stop and restart. Tailnet HTTPS, bearer authorization and upstream provider completion remain separate gaps.

- **unit, recorded**: [overalls:underclass_monitor_local_readiness:ratio5m](repo:nix/tests/evaluation/otel-slo-rules.nix) — Promtool checks a two-host zero, missing host, two missed scrapes and a retained recording after a host disappears.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [underclass-monitor-probe.service](repo:nix/tests/machines/otel-fleet/default.nix) — Disposable two-node pinned Underclass and Rust probe reach the isolated OTLP collector; stopping and restarting the monitor changes the exported gauge and a complete five-minute recording falls below one.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).

## Read guarded application duration baselines in private Grafana

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, transport, recovery.

Boundary: Loopback Grafana authenticates the operator and reads only the nine Nix-provisioned candidate duration recordings; source-owned histograms enter the collector under assigned application provenance.

Required refusal: Wrong Grafana credentials are refused. Zero current admitted traffic, stale source markers, absent completion ratio, any admitted request without a duration, or duration beyond the last finite bucket removes the p95 candidate instead of showing a reassuring latency value.

Tested scope: Prometheus rule fixtures verify one numeric p95 and refusal of partial duration, overrange, retained-recording warmup and zero-traffic windows. A two-node NixOS check boots Grafana and checks all 21 provisioned panels, including first and last guarded latency queries. The earlier production deployment confirmed nine healthy rules, 20 authenticated panels and three guarded p95 series after warmup; the new Underclass panel awaits deployment. Operator browser rendering, end-to-end workflow latency, and 28-day targets remain unverified.

- **unit, recorded**: [overalls:brady_candidate_duration:p95_5m](repo:nix/tests/evaluation/otel-slo-rules.nix) — Promtool verifies the expected p95 with complete in-range request durations and no output for missing traffic, partial duration coverage, an overrange window, retained p95 during warmup or retained p95 with zero current traffic.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [api/dashboards/uid/overalls-otel-baseline](repo:nix/tests/machines/otel-fleet/default.nix) — Initial expanded two-node Grafana run verified 36 panels including thirteen provisional objectives and the durable proxy candidate. This records panel provisioning and guarded numeric transport, not production history, provider completion or the subsequently added direct-ingress refusal assertion. Historical 33-panel evidence remains in the provisional seven-day review.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-02-underclass-proxy-slo.md).
- **manual, recorded**: [all nine candidate duration recordings with `health=ok`](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md) — Read-only Overalls Prometheus rules API and authenticated loopback Grafana API after revision 46435eac reached healthy; three guarded p95 series appeared after warmup. Confirms rule and panel provisioning plus one measured snapshot, not browser rendering or baseline compliance.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).

**Gaps:** Production operator rendering and 28-day baseline remain open; candidate source durations have different workflow boundaries and may omit failures with unknown duration.

**Preferred test work:** Measure duration coverage and volume over the baseline, then define per-journey latency targets with native user-path evidence.

## Observe an admitted Brady web request as a candidate service level indicator

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, transport, isolation, recovery.

Boundary: Lilly-resolved attribution and a terminal Brady outcome enter fixed cumulative counters; a private file is checked by a Rust host exporter before separate local OTLP collection.

Required refusal: Unresolved attribution, policy refusal, malformed request, stale or foreign snapshot and nonmonotonic counters must not improve the candidate success ratio.

Tested scope: Source arithmetic and Rust snapshot properties; the two-node collector fixture uses a synthesized snapshot. A separate native fleet run passed real attributed guest requests through the Rust exporter to the collector but failed later in restart recovery.

- **property, recorded**: [generated admitted outcomes have closed cumulative SLO buckets](repo:brady/test/metrics_test.exs) — Generated resolved terminal outcomes, refusals and fixed duration buckets.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **property, recorded**: [generated_outcome_arithmetic_and_monotonicity](repo:nix/modules/otel-guest/receiver/src/slo.rs) — Generated snapshot arithmetic, monotonicity and fixed OTLP metric names.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [overalls_outer_brady_requests_total](repo:nix/tests/machines/otel-fleet/default.nix) — Two-node collector fixture exports a synthetic validated Brady snapshot with restricted Unix peer permission and collector-assigned labels.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [overalls_outer_brady_requests_total](repo:nix/tests/machines/fleet/scenarios/brady.py) — Real Firecracker guest requests and Lilly attribution produced a source-owned private SLO snapshot and exported it through Rust to the collector; the complete test later failed in restart recovery.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).

**Gaps:** The full native Brady fleet gate still fails in restart recovery; native malformed-request refusal through the collector and an independent production baseline remain absent. No compliance rule consumes this candidate series.

**Preferred test work:** Resolve the later restart recovery timeout, verify a native refusal leaves the collector series unchanged, then measure a target baseline.

## Observe a registered mirror refresh as a candidate service level indicator

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: authorization, transport, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Enrico's manager counts unique admitted terminal refreshes and exposes fixed cumulative outcomes and duration through its owner-only Unix health socket; Rust exports through an Enrico-only collector intake with Nix-owned service identity.

Required refusal: Invalid, busy and duplicate attempts do not add outcomes; unauthenticated reads cannot access the mirror; ordinary Enrico UID cannot connect to the collector socket; repository labels never enter SLO metrics.

Tested scope: Generated arithmetic and packaged NixOS mirror journey covering successful, duplicate and failed refreshes, local Git readback, source outage, Rust export and collector socket refusal.

- **machine, recorded**: [Rust exporter delivers Enrico-owned terminal outcomes to the isolated collector](repo:nix/tests/machines/enrico-service/driver.py) — Packaged Enrico with local Git fixture, terminal success and failure, duplicate exclusion, Rust OTLP export and ordinary UID collector socket refusal.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).

**Gaps:** Cross-host Marshall scheduling, manager-crash accounting, production collection and a measured final target baseline remain open; the provisional seven-day rule consumes only the named candidate series.

**Preferred test work:** Exercise Marshall-to-Enrico scheduling and manager restart under native NixOS, then verify live collection and measure the production baseline.

## Observe a completed Marshall browser HTTP response as a candidate transport indicator

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, transport, isolation, recovery.

Boundary: Marshall counts endpoint responses only after the privacy, health and public machine-API prefilters; a mode-0640 cumulative snapshot in a dynamic-user-owned, mode-0750 directory feeds a static Rust exporter using the Marshall-only collector socket with a Nix-configured replica identity.

Required refusal: Health probes and early machine-API refusals do not enter the denominator; request paths, credentials and user identity cannot become labels; the exporter cannot replace the source snapshot through directory permissions, and ordinary Marshall UID lacks direct collector socket group access.

Tested scope: Generated status and latency-bucket arithmetic, real in-process endpoint health exclusion and 404 completion, a synthetic two-node collector fixture, a packaged Marshall HTTPS response exported through Rust, and one production public HTTPS 404 whose application-origin request and success counters rose from zero to one and reached central Prometheus. The source counter later reset on restart.

- **machine, recorded**: [real admitted browser request survives producer loss as one failure without invented duration](repo:nix/tests/machines/otel-marshall/default.nix) — Current packaged verified-TLS HTTP response and independent Rust collector export, source UID/intake and exporter-write refusal, recorder SIGKILL without application restart, clean deployment coverage/totals continuity, and admitted producer loss with exactly one failure and no invented duration. The same full gate passes retired-page HTTP404 and current/remembered browser revocation. No exact signed-in response-at-collector, physical power-loss, production-history or notification claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-09-observability-decoupling.md).
- **property, recorded**: [candidate browser outcomes retain closed counts and fixed buckets](repo:marshall/test/marshall/slo_test.exs) — Generated HTTP status partition, fixed buckets and cumulative arithmetic.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [actual endpoint excludes health and counts a completed browser response](repo:marshall/test/marshall/slo_test.exs) — In-process Marshall endpoint call distinguishes health from a completed 404 response; it does not exercise packaged TLS or collector export.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [overalls_outer_marshall_web_requests_total](repo:nix/tests/machines/otel-fleet/default.nix) — Two-node fixture exports a synthetic Marshall snapshot from a dynamic UID through a mode-0750 source directory and static Rust exporter with fixed instance identity; exporter directory-write and ordinary source UID collector-socket access are refused. The source endpoint is not packaged in this fixture.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [packaged-marshall-request-to-collector](repo:nix/tests/machines/otel-marshall/default.nix) — A packaged Marshall HTTPS 404 increments its private counter and reaches the central collector through the static Rust exporter with fixed Overalls identity; directory-write and direct collector-socket refusals pass. Disposable WireGuard peers; no signed-in browser or restart claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-28-fleet-otel-baseline.md).

**Gaps:** The current native browser retirement/refusal journey passes, but its own signed-in completed response has not been asserted at the collector. Native recorder loss, clean application restart and admitted producer loss preserve durable identity and settled totals; physical power loss, measured production targets and seven-day compliance remain open. VM creation completion is a separate journey.

**Preferred test work:** Assert exact signed-in browser response export through the collector, then measure the live baseline before selecting a target; retain the passing native recorder/application-loss conservation oracles.

## An operator starts Lilly with a private runtime configuration

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, execution.

Boundary: Operator-owned private configuration is validated before Lilly creates runtime authority or status listeners.

Tested scope: Thirteen generated invalid file/schema/config classes in four shuffled histories, each starting actual OTP in a separate test-build BEAM, plus an unset-config no-runtime control.

- **property, recorded**: [generated invalid configurations reject fresh OTP boots before serving](repo:lilly/test/lilly/control/invalid_boot_property_test.exs) — 52 fresh OTP rejections across thirteen invalid classes, no traced named supervisor/Runtime/StatusServer start attempt and no surviving supervisor/Runtime/status/terminal-metrics sockets, unchanged files/runtime directories, no started event and no synthetic credential in captured output. Valid loader precheck; no configured native execution claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-29-antithesis-property-audit.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [an unset configuration starts only the deliberate unconfigured application](repo:lilly/test/lilly/control/invalid_boot_property_test.exs) — Separate BEAM starts Lilly supervisor with no configured Runtime or status sockets; does not count as invalid-config rejection.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-09-29-antithesis-property-audit.md).

## An operator starts Galileo with a private control credential and local integration socket

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, transport, execution.

## Retire old terminal peer owners when their admission budget restarts

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: transport, recovery.

Boundary: Incoming/outgoing peers share a process-owned budget and must retire when that accounting incarnation dies; a successor cannot retain live unaccounted peers.

Required refusal: Old peer owners must terminate after budget death. The replacement starts empty and admits exactly the requested replacement pairs while the synthetic host stream remains alive.

Tested scope: Generated one to three real verified-TLS peer pairs and persistent attachments through production signed current-location proof validation. Exact owner retirement, successor occupancy and fresh pair admission are checked; host stream/replies are synthetic.

- **property, recorded**: [budget restart retires live TLS peers and admits exact replacement pairs](repo:marshall/test/marshall/terminal/remote_test.exs) — Matching isolated fork with real verified TLS and production peer-proof API, synthetic host stream and attachments; shrinking is disabled for destructive restart histories.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-06-terminal-peer-budget-incarnation.md).

**Gaps:** No native guest survival, physical channel custody, independent-node partition or hosted acceptance. Wrong-budget-PID mutant was caught at old owner retirement and restored four-check execution passed; full Marshall gate/typing remain pending.

**Preferred test work:** Finish restored/full component verification, then repeat budget-incarnation loss with native guest attachments and independent nodes.

## Release a terminal peer attachment when its remote frontend stops responding

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: transport, recovery.

Boundary: Peer activity renews a fifteen-second silence budget; outgoing ping cadence must not postpone retirement beyond that budget.

Required refusal: A silent outgoing hop must retire and notify its viewer; the incoming application must release its budget slot after resumption.

Tested scope: Generated real verified-TLS peer histories on both sides with activity between heartbeat ticks, independently renewed directory authority, synthetic host stream and attachments, fifteen-second retirement with250ms scheduler allowance. Incoming cleanup timestamps the actual budget-release cast.

## Recover the terminal host stream after an opening worker dies

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: transport, recovery.

Boundary: Lilly owns each linked/monitored opening worker; worker death cannot occupy an attempt forever or confer stream readiness, and abrupt stream-owner death retires its worker.

Required refusal: A pending handshake remains unready; only a hello response enables readiness. Stream shutdown closes the opening socket without guest effects.

Tested scope: Generated one to three peer-observed worker deaths, fresh real loopback handshakes, exact original host/session/epoch hello, readiness transition/loss, and both orderly and abrupt owner shutdown during a later stalled handshake.

- **property, recorded**: [registered opening sockets retire on worker loss or failed completion](repo:lilly/test/terminal/opening_socket_property_test.exs) — Scripted worker installed with actual stream-owned links/monitors, passive loopback sockets and real registration/retirement callbacks; not production dial reach, TLS or native guests.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-06-opening-socket-custody.md).

## See where a guest boot spends its time and boot faster without weakening boot-bound trust

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, transport, recovery.

Boundary: The host boot-log writer stamps console lines without interpreting payloads under its 1 MiB cap; the guest boot report is observation only. Ubuntu skips update-ca-certificates only when the marker, managed root bytes, bundle PEM blocks and per-root links prove the store already reflects the exact delivered set; every boot still delivers and verifies the current host set before networking (ADR-0113, ADR-0154).

Required refusal: Any marker mismatch, changed, extra or missing managed root, missing bundle block or link, or failed rebuild takes the full replace path: the marker is withdrawn before mutation and written only after update-ca-certificates verifies. A retired root fails bundle and CApath verification after the next boot.

Tested scope: Hegel properties prove chunking-independent stamping and exact bounded retention. Rust unit cases cover the receiver's no-op, rotation, drift and failed-rebuild paths. Native boot-profile journeys on NixOS and Ubuntu boot through Lilly's real trust delivery with identical, added and retired root sets, verifying the guest bundle (and Ubuntu CApath links) after each boot.

- **property, recorded**: [stamping_is_chunking_independent](repo:lilly/native/boot-log/src/tests.rs) — Generated console bytes and chunkings stamp identically to the whole buffer and to an independent line oracle; prefix stripping recovers the input. Pure writer logic; no VMM or guest.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-10-guest-boot-profile.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [ubuntu_rotation_and_every_drift_take_the_full_path](repo:lyra/src/bin/overalls-trustd.rs) — Temporary-store receiver cases with an update-ca-certificates emulator: identical delivery is a no-op, rotation and five drift kinds rebuild, a failed rebuild withdraws the marker. Does not run the real Ubuntu tool.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-10-guest-boot-profile.md).
- **machine, recorded**: [a restart retiring that root removes it from the guest trust store](repo:nix/tests/machines/zfs-guest/boot-profile.py) — Real Firecracker NixOS and Ubuntu guests booted three times through Lilly trust delivery; boot report printed each boot; bundle membership after each delivery and Ubuntu CApath accept/refuse for present/retired roots. Nested KVM, isolated from Audi.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-10-guest-boot-profile.md).

**Gaps:** Nested-KVM 1-vCPU fixture timings only; no fleet-hardware measurement. ADR-0154's accepted at-most-30-second completion wait is unimplemented across in-flight tool timeouts; a controlled runner waited 40.25s, requiring a timeout-budget correction/regression. Isolated fixtures cannot finish booting without Audi, so systemd-analyze time is observed only on the NixOS image without private services. No curl verification against a platform-issued leaf certificate in this journey.

**Preferred test work:** Enforce the accepted completion-wait bound with a scoped timeout regression, record boot reports from fleet NixOS and Ubuntu creates after deployment, and add an issued-leaf TLS check to the rotation journey.

## Step up an administrator inline and continue the exact pending action

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: A current stored login and persisted administrator role remain separate from a fixed fifteen-minute database-time passkey receipt. One five-minute socket-local continuation names the exact actor, login, route/resource binding, action and canonical arguments; current Cedar and domain preconditions decide the retried mutation. No-key setup keeps only an allowlisted Security return path and requires explicit resubmission.

Required refusal: Refuse ordinary-user mutation, missing or stale role, absent or expired receipt, missing first-enrollment email freshness, failed or replayed ceremony, substituted arguments, another socket's continuation, invalid current resource/generation, revoked login, proving-key removal and elevated foreign Customer access. Staging and refused actions leave privileged mutation and target-action audit rows unchanged; failed assertions may consume their challenge, and successful verification retains its separate security audit even if target admission refuses.

Tested scope: The current implementation has the fifteen-minute receipt, enrolled-key inline modal, socket-local normalized continuation, no-key Security return path and expired-target recovery. Earlier focused backend and recovery runs retain their captured scopes. Exact pushed d4bd0214 Marshall precommit passed 1,474 Elixir checks (237 properties, 1,237 tests), four excluded; 32 terminal and eight repository/MFA browser tests, workspace TypeScript and forced Dialyzer passed. This includes first-enrollment explicit retry and five recovery examples. Exact pushed packaged mfa-login/admin-account checks passed ordinary/remembered reads, no-key return/retry, inline continuation, cancellation/focus, persisted 900-second receipt, role refusal, cross-replica revocation and expired challenge refusal after upstream Push 3. Native journeys do not exercise VM recovery. Earlier 1722fbdf final-source capture, initial pre-final and historical five-minute/redirect results retain their separate scopes.

- **property, recorded**: [elevation expires exactly at fifteen minutes and rejects future verification](repo:marshall/test/marshall/accounts/security_test.exs) — Generated timestamp arithmetic checks the fixed 900-second receipt and future-time refusal; no native database clock or browser timer acceptance.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-11-privileged-administrator-step-up.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [button metadata is discarded and the exact action continues once](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/admin_elevation_test.exs) — Mounted LiveView with real signed WebAuthn assertion executes one server-staged role change and refuses assertion replay without another target audit entry; browser button metadata is discarded during canonicalization.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-11-privileged-administrator-step-up.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [another socket's nonce cannot continue the action](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/admin_elevation_test.exs) — Separate LiveView sockets using one stored login cannot exchange a pending nonce. Companion examples cover cancellation and navigation without privileged mutation.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-11-privileged-administrator-step-up.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [a retired target is refused by the current domain state after verification](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/admin_elevation_test.exs) — After successful proof, current domain admission refuses a retired target without granting its role or writing a target mutation audit. Separate examples fence demotion, login revocation and passkey-generation change.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-11-privileged-administrator-step-up.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [expired or invalid assertions refuse, and a fresh challenge can retry](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/admin_elevation_test.exs) — SQL-expired and wrong-origin signed assertions refuse the captured action; a new valid challenge can resume that same server-held submission. Grammar and binding examples reject unknown arguments, changed identity and five-minute intent expiry.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-11-privileged-administrator-step-up.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [first enrollment returns to the original page for an explicit retry](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/security_live_test.exs) — No-key admin redirects to Security, enrolls via a signed ceremony and receives the allowlisted original-page link without executing the role change.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-11-privileged-administrator-step-up.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [cancel before begin reply prevents ceremony and stale reply cannot change a new attempt](repo:marshall/test/browser/admin_elevation_test.mjs) — Simulated DOM and authenticator exercise the actual typed hook's challenge validation, nonce-only transport, cancellation/reopen and disconnect/late-result fences. No real focus trap, browser TLS or native WebAuthn claim.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-11-privileged-administrator-step-up.md).

## Start an isolated control fixture and establish one truthful Antithesis setup signal

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Disposable PostgreSQL, migration, two Marshall HTTPS processes, reviewed Lilly enrollment and a fresh connected host report precede the sole workload setup signal. A SELECT-only TLS oracle checks committed migration, positive report sequence, a timestamp within 60 seconds and a durable session matching current session ID, host ID and epoch. Execution and real Control probes enforce wire v4 separately; SQL does not infer a protocol version. Hosted controlled pools report unavailable capacity and false execution readiness.

Required refusal: Missing or inconsistent synthetic credentials, absent migration, failed TLS or HTTPS readiness, unavailable SQL, stale or disconnected host reports and timeout refuse setup. A controlled command marker cannot prove guest boot, native storage, stop, delete or cleanup; guest callbacks remain unavailable.

Tested scope: An earlier local Compose diagnostic started PostgreSQL/migration, Marshall A/B, enrollment and Lilly health and created three synthetic users/passkeys, but its connected v4 host had report_sequence 0 and setup was withheld. A later fresh project emitted setup after a connected, fresh v4 report at sequence 44. Six serial Control commands exited zero and emitted 13 true SDK assertion records; later SQL found fresh sequence 392, a report counter rather than command or physical capacity count. Its selected manifest has four stale runtime-dependency hashes, so the local image execution is not a frozen hosted baseline. The 104-item catalog has no fully implemented hosted property. Separate TCG records are outside this control setup.

This does not execute PostgreSQL or establish fresh composed acceptance; historical diagnostic4 execution predates this witness..
- **unit, present**: [def test_setup_emitted_once_after_ready_probe](repo:antithesis/workload/tests/test_bootstrap.py) — Unit source substitutes a ready probe and checks one setup call; no actual PostgreSQL, HTTPS, Control or hosted ingestion..
- **unit, present**: [def test_unavailable_oracle_does_not_look_like_empty_result](repo:antithesis/workload/tests/test_bootstrap.py) — Unit source checks unavailable SQL and non-SELECT refusal without a live database..
- **unit, present**: [test "controlled publication is durable but cannot settle a physical outcome"](repo:antithesis/lilly/test/controlled_executor_test.exs) — Focused local controlled-adapter source checks publication versus physical receipt; no native guest or kernel effect..
- **unit, present**: [test "known short write poisons the sink and prevents concatenation"](repo:antithesis/sdk/test/sdk_test.exs) — Local SDK writer source injects an uncertain short write; hosted output ingestion remains unverified..
- **manual, recorded**: ["admin_a" => encode_account(admin_a)](repo:antithesis/operator/fixture.ex) — Clean local Compose diagnostic: exactly three users and passkeys by read-only SQL, no enrollment listener, connected host with zero reports; setup withheld.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-13-antithesis-harness-foundation.md).
- **manual, recorded**: [Six packaged Control serial commands exited zero and emitted 13 true SDK](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-13-antithesis-harness-foundation.md) — Historical local diagnostic4 selected-image execution: setup journal signal after fresh v4 sequence 44, six packaged serial Control commands with 13 true SDK JSONL assertions, and later fresh sequence 392 by read-only SQL. This predates the current durable-session SQL witness; four selected-manifest runtime-dependency hashes are stale. No hosted ingestion, native capacity or guest effect.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-13-antithesis-harness-foundation.md).

**Gaps:** The controlled container committed cgroup-derived effective fields, but raw cgroup files were not independently compared. Pool capacity remains nil and execution readiness false. The selected manifest is stale; retained-volume recovery and hosted ingestion are unverified. BEAM fallback has no branch coverage, Python catalog scanning is not source coverage, and native Rust instrumentation is pending. KVM and physical isolation need separate machine evidence.

**Preferred test work:** Freeze the corrected selected manifest against actual image/source identity, independently compare measured cgroup files with committed fields, exercise retained-volume recovery and record hosted SDK ingestion. Add native NixOS and generated property evidence for guest effects.

## Boot a disposable NixOS guest in the local Antithesis emulator and retain its disk identity across restart

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: A local QEMU TCG runner boots the production NixOS guest services in a q35-adapted image. The port 1024 readiness response is checked exactly; the same qcow2 overlay is reused across a stopped restart, and retained machine-id bytes are inspected offline. Runner process identities and TAP cleanup bound the local fixture.

Required refusal: The initial q35 image reached multi-user and answered port 1026 flush but did not report readiness when its NIC appeared as enp0s3; the net.ifnames=0 repair allowed the exact readiness check. A flush reply alone cannot authorize readiness, and an emulator boot cannot authorize hosted Lilly placement or native physical effects.

Tested scope: The local guest-boot record identifies the image, runner, tool configuration, source hashes and three ready epochs. Epoch 4 reused the qcow2 overlay; an offline read matched retained machine-id bytes, qemu-img check found zero errors, and owned processes/TAP were removed. The foundation review reconciles this passing manual software-emulator scope separately from the later passing base control-core setup; no integrated guest journey follows.

- **manual, recorded**: [Exact port 1024 readiness and port 1026 flush succeeded](repo:antithesis/verification/guest-boot.md) — Local QEMU TCG boot, same-overlay restart and offline retained-disk inspection; the JSON record binds exact image, runner, tools and source identities. No Lilly callback, native KVM/Firecracker/ZFS or hosted run.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-13-antithesis-harness-foundation.md).

**Gaps:** Default hosted Lilly still refuses guest effects, while the optional integrated GuestExecutor has no composed journey. A separate inner-host TCG boot measured ZFS/cgroups but did not connect Control or admit a guest. No workspace delivery, Firecracker/KVM, physical isolation or hosted baseline is established by this guest boot. The router has a separate local TCG result. This manual check lacks preferred NixOS machine/property evidence for the integrated guest bridge.

**Preferred test work:** Execute the selected integrated guest adapter with measured ownership and capacity, add a NixOS machine journey and generated recovery properties, then record a separate hosted execution identity.

## Boot the optional inner Lilly host and observe real ZFS and cgroup capacity before enrollment

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: A selected QEMU TCG NixOS Lilly host prepares a fixed ZFS member and samples pool/dataset GUIDs, capacity, and kernel cgroup CPU/memory. The private 9p witness identifies an image but does not grant Control authority. Enrollment and current authenticated reports must precede admission.

Required refusal: Unavailable physical health, absent private credential, mismatched host/pool identity, disconnected Control or false execution readiness cannot be treated as schedulable capacity. A positive ZFS sample alone cannot prove guest readiness, native KVM isolation or crash recovery.

Tested scope: The captured record preserves two stopped boots with unavailable health and exact third and fourth images that booted and emitted real ZFS/cgroup values. The fourth private-service host completed runtime PKI preparation and returned a workspace-source-v2 guest image witness. Its host credential, pool ID and CA were issued, but its application rejected runtime configuration before authenticated Control; the stopped-root journal recorded two invalid-configuration errors. Both measured samples remained disconnected and execution-unready; the first three image identities had private 9p witnesses. No authenticated Control, admitted nested guest or actual QEMU crash was recorded.
