**Gaps:** The fourth private host image built and booted, but its native ZFS root compression policy and IPv4 forwarding were missing, and Lilly rejected runtime configuration before Control. Inner Ziggy rotation refused on the disabled host; no private role credential was issued. GuestTrust receipt and crash-actuator focused tests do not establish private service, terminal, adversarial network isolation or recovery execution. The full integrated-mode suite failed at 7/559. Native NixOS machine/property evidence and a hosted baseline remain absent.

**Preferred test work:** Run the selected private Docker topology at the built image identity, then record reviewed enrollment, current authenticated Control/report, a real nested guest and crash recovery; retain separate native NixOS machine and generated property evidence.

## Build the Windows Server 2025 evaluation image offline and boot it under the patched Firecracker to the serial Special Administration Console

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: The image is installed inside a KVM Nix sandbox from Microsoft's hash-pinned evaluation ISO with restricted user networking and pinned virtio-win drivers; Firecracker boots the edk2 CloudHv firmware as a PVH payload over the opt-in virtio-pci transport. The patched VMM keeps its 1.16.1 identity and static linking; its seccomp surface widens by exactly one vCPU-thread ioctl (KVM_IOEVENTFD) for BAR relocation. This scenario covers the operator launcher only (no jailer, no host network guard); the Lilly launch path is the separate windows-lilly-guest-launch scenario.

Required refusal: The build fails without the OVERALLS-SETUP-COMPLETE serial marker and the firmware build fails without the PVH note or the patched aperture. The guest has a bootstrap Administrator credential and an interactive SAC login on serial, which must not be mistaken for the Linux console boundary. The manifest carries exactly the eight version-5 keys; a display name or any version-4 field is refused by the protocol.

Tested scope: Patched VMM identity, firmware provenance and offline assembly rules are checked without installing Windows; the nine VMM patches carry unit tests (PVH RSDP, PM timer, Hyper-V leaves, CMOS clock, BAR sizing and relocation with doorbell moves, virtio reset and re-activation, scatter-gather block requests on both I/O engines, vsock indirect descriptor tables). Recorded on 2026-10-13: the offline installation completed in about 255 seconds of guest time with all nine serial checkpoints, and the image booted under the patched Firecracker with both firmware variants to the Windows SAC prompt, answering `id` (OVERALLS, build 26100, Windows Server) and `i` (one virtio NIC). Recorded on 2026-10-14: the windows-guest machine test passed on nested KVM (firmware banner after 253 s of guest time, SAC> 6 s later, 274 s in total).

- **unit, present**: [Firecracker v1.16.1](repo:nix/tests/evaluation/firecracker-vmm.nix) — The patched VMM reports the upstream version, lists --enable-pci and remains a static x86-64 firecracker/jailer pair; it does not boot a guest..
- **unit, present**: [disable-legacy=on](repo:nix/tests/evaluation/windows-guest.nix) — Builder rules: KVM sandbox, restricted networking, modern-only virtio-pci, pinned ISO and drivers, EMS enabled, no autologon, version-5 manifest shape, launcher flags. No installation or boot..
- **machine, recorded**: [SAC>](repo:nix/tests/machines/windows-guest/driver.py) — Installs the evaluation image and boots it under the patched Firecracker on nested KVM until the Windows SAC prompt appears on the serial log. Firmware entry, ACPI, PM timer, virtio-blk-pci and EMS only; no networking or agents. Passed on 2026-10-14 (273.9 s script time).; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-14-windows-lilly-launch.md).

**Gaps:** No Marshall-driven Windows fleet matrix is recorded. The named image and nested KVM boot prove the operator and firmware paths only; WSL 2 and Podman nested virtualization, Hyper-V synthetic timers, SAC login and timekeeping under load remain unverified. Lilly launch, MMDS identity, agent trust/readiness, sparse template handling and Windows tools are covered by the linked scenarios.

**Preferred test work:** Add the Marshall-driven multi-OS fleet journey and record WSL 2, timekeeping and SAC security evidence; retain the operator-only boundary for this scenario.

## Install the overalls CLI on a Windows workstation from the machine page and SSH into Linux and Windows guests through Audi

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Marshall serves /bin/install.ps1 templated with the deployment's SSH host trust (503 without it) and the windows-amd64 artifact with its SHA-256 digest from the same manifest as the Unix targets. The installer validates origin, zone and host against the issued values, requires an OpenSSH client with hostname protection (9.6+) and offers winget when the in-box 9.5 client is older, verifies the digest, runs config-ssh --staged-installer with the downloaded binary before installing it, and connects with overalls ssh through the recorded client. The managed SSH block quotes ProxyCommand as one double-quoted absolute path because Win32-OpenSSH runs it without a shell. Updates use the same digest manifest and activate by renaming the running executable aside.

Required refusal: A non-https origin, a foreign management host or zone, or a host outside the zone is refused before any download; a digest mismatch or failed SSH configuration leaves the installed binary and SSH profile unchanged; a client without hostname protection stops setup; an executable path containing a double quote is refused for ProxyCommand.

Tested scope: Rust unit tests pin Windows ProxyCommand quoting, the windows-amd64 target name and the portable UTC date; the Lyra Nix check type-checks the overalls binary for x86_64-pc-windows-gnu; the release publishes a PE32+ windows-amd64 artifact in the manifest; a Nix check parses install.ps1 with PowerShell and pins its refusals; Marshall tests cover /bin/install.ps1 templating and the machine page's Windows section.

- **unit, present**: [windows_proxy_command_is_one_double_quoted_path](repo:lyra/src/ssh_config.rs) — ProxyCommand quoting for Win32-OpenSSH: one double-quoted path, percent doubled, quotes and control characters refused; no SSH client runs..
- **unit, present**: [PowerShell installer is deployment bound, verifies the digest and gates OpenSSH 9.6](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/controllers/overalls_controller_test.exs) — Marshall renders /bin/install.ps1 with the deployment host and zone, no-store and without cookies, and refuses it without trust; no PowerShell runs..
- **unit, present**: [origin does not match this installer](repo:nix/tests/overalls-install-ps1.nix) — PowerShell parses the installer and it refuses bad origins, hosts and zones before any network access; no Windows host or OpenSSH..

**Gaps:** No execution on a real Windows workstation yet: installation with winget-installed OpenSSH, config-ssh against Porsche, overalls ssh into Linux and Windows guests, overalls open and an update activation by rename-aside are unexercised; the planned proof uses a Windows Server 2025 fleet VM as the workstation.

**Preferred test work:** Run the page's PowerShell command on a Windows Server 2025 fleet VM, connect to a Linux and a Windows guest, and observe an update activation; record it in the review.

## Give Windows guests an agent set with a capability table, reach a guest-side listener over virtio-vsock through the viosock driver, and make the fleet ready to stage the image

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: OverallsProtocol.GuestAgents names the closed agent set (linux, none, windows-v1) and a pure capability table; Lilly's jail, driver and executor and Marshall's per-capability image facts branch on the table, never on the set's name, so the agentless none row reproduces ADR-0170 exactly. The firmware jail gets the jail-local vsock endpoint only for an agent set that speaks the Lilly protocols. The readiness budget is the agent set's (600 s for windows-v1) and is recorded once per boot. The image ships the virtio-win viosock driver and, until the real agents exist, a SYSTEM probe that discovers the provider's address family at runtime, listens on vsock port 1099 and answers the host's CONNECT with one JSON line, publishing only its own log on the EFI system partition. Windows binaries are cross-built from the shared Lyra crate with the pinned zig toolchain; the Lyra gate type-checks the library and the Windows bins for x86_64-pc-windows-gnu. Images are configured as { path } objects; a size check decides template cacheability and every root disk is copied on demand (ADR-0177); both hosts' template budget is 96 GiB.

Required refusal: An unknown agent set string, a workspace source version 2 on an agentless manifest or a ninth manifest key is refused by the protocol; an older Lilly refuses a windows-v1 bundle, so the converged push precedes any placement. A missing, corrupt or mismatched manifest yields every capability false in Marshall. A bare image path or any image key beyond path, including the retired standby key, refuses Lilly's configuration. The probe carries no identity or credential material. The editor and the Inception profile stay refused for windows-v1.

Tested scope: Protocol tests pin the capability table and the windows-v1 manifest and bundle vectors; Lilly tests pin the agentless jail configuration, the vsock addition for windows-v1, the recorded readiness budget, the image configuration shape and template cacheability; Marshall tests bind the capabilities to the exact image ID for every agent set and exercise the per-capability surfaces; the windows-guest-unit evaluation checks the viosock driver, the agents directory, the probe task, the manifest arguments and the launcher's vsock device; the lyra check builds and type-checks the Windows target. Recorded on 2026-10-14: lyraWindows built overalls-winprobe.exe (PE32+, 320 000 bytes) through zig. The windows-guest machine test dials the probe through the launcher's vsock socket after the SAC prompt and reads its published log.

- **unit, present**: [windows_v1](repo:protocol/test/guest_agents_test.exs) — The agent set wire values, the capability table and the readiness budgets are closed and pinned; no manifest or guest is involved..
- **unit, present**: [windows_v1](repo:lilly/test/vm/jail_test.exs) — The agentless firmware jail configuration is unchanged and windows-v1 adds exactly the vsock key; no Firecracker process runs..
- **unit, present**: [cacheability is the artifact size against the pool budget, without hashing](repo:lilly/test/host/image_template_cacheable_test.exs) — Cacheability is a size check against the pool budget on staged images; no pool or dataset exists..
- **property, present**: [windows-v1](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/image_guest_agents_capability_test.exs) — Capabilities are bound to the validated manifest and its exact image ID for both firmware agent sets and fail closed otherwise; sandboxed Ash resources only..
- **unit, present**: [viosock](repo:nix/tests/evaluation/windows-guest.nix) — Builder rules: viosock in the driver tree, the agents directory and probe task only when agents are supplied, manifest agent set and workspace source version from the builder arguments, the launcher's vsock device. No installation or boot..
- **machine, recorded**: [overalls-winprobe](repo:nix/tests/machines/windows-guest/driver.py) — Installs the image with the viosock driver and the probe, boots it under the patched Firecracker on nested KVM to the SAC prompt, dials vsock port 1099 through the launcher's Unix socket, asserts the probe's JSON answer names host CID 2, and reads the probe's published log from the EFI system partition. Operator launcher only; no Lilly, no agents. Passed on 2026-10-14 (318.8 s script time; probe answered 23.7 s after the SAC prompt with address family 40 via the control device).; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-14-windows-guest-agents.md).
- **unit, present**: [validation_binds_boot_digest_and_certificate_shape](repo:lyra/src/guest/trust.rs) — Shared trust rules: frames are bounded and round-trip, a request is accepted only for the exact boot with a matching digest and well-formed unique certificates, and the bundle is assembled from the base roots; the MMDS client's token-per-fetch exchange and document validation and the readiness line's six keys are covered beside it. Rust unit tests in the Lyra check..
- **unit, present**: [authorized keys are pulled per account and follow key removal](repo:marshall/test/marshall/ssh/guest_access_test.exs) — The pulled key list names the roster's writers with exactly their unarchived registered keys, drops a removed key on the next pull, refuses a foreign or bootstrap binding and a bad credential, and records no login. Sandboxed Ash resources; Ziggy's route tests refuse the bootstrap binding on the new path..
- **unit, present**: [reconcile_activates_deactivates_and_journals](repo:lyra/src/guest/ssh_roster.rs) — Shared roster rules with a fake account backend: names fit both platforms, stale and diverging rosters are refused without mutation, departed accounts are disabled before new ones are activated, and a failed activation leaves the staged union in the state..
- **machine, recorded**: [ready_answered](repo:nix/tests/machines/windows-lilly-guest/driver.py) — The windows-v1 image through the real Lilly driver on nested KVM: the jail carries the vsock device, the guest's trust daemon installs the delivered roots and answers the installed receipt with a boot UUID, provisioning reaches ready with the same UUID within the 600 s budget, the unattested commit is refused, terminald authenticates the boot over vsock, lists root first, refuses an account the roster never created and runs a root PowerShell under ConPTY that prints a computed marker, a workspace delivery is recorded settled for the boot, port 22 stays closed without a roster, Inception stays refused, and a restart from the portable bundle yields a different boot UUID. Passed on 2026-10-15 in 2863 s on the reference-guarded image. No roster SSH, roster-user terminal, clone or agent turn.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-14-windows-guest-agents.md).

**Gaps:** Fleet proofs on 2026-10-15 cover creation to ready in seconds, trust, roster accounts and sshd, the host certificate, executor registration, a public workspace clone and a relocation between hosts (review). Still unproven: an SSH login through Audi, a roster-user ConPTY terminal under an S4U logon and an agent turn (they need a browser-approved key), crash restart of the scheduled-task agents, the pool-level refill with an uncacheable image configured, and no Marshall-driven Windows fleet test makes any of the fleet proofs repeatable; ConPTY re-renders terminal output.

**Preferred test work:** Record the browser-approved proofs (SSH through Audi, a roster-user terminal, an agent turn) and add a Marshall-driven Windows fleet test so the fleet proofs become machine tests.

## Place the Windows image through Lilly as a guest without agents and reach it on its delivered /32

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: The protocol admits exactly the eight-key version-5 manifest and the seven-key version-2 firmware bundle beside the unchanged version-4 and version-1 shapes; artifact names come from the protocol only. Lilly keeps rootfs.ext4 as the pool-side root slot, bind-mounts the firmware read-only, seeds the boot identity (placement, execution epoch, boot challenge, /32, gateway, MAC; never the generation) into Firecracker's MMDS from a root-owned jail-local metadata.json before launch, boots /firmware with the Hyper-V CPU template and --enable-pci, and runs the guest under the jailer, seccomp observation and the per-tap guard. The guest's SYSTEM bootstrap task reads MMDS with a V2 session token, validates every field including the adapter MAC and applies exactly the delivered /32. Marshall projects per-capability guest facts (guest_capabilities) from the immutable image, all false for the agentless set.

Required refusal: A bundle with guest_agents none never reports guest_ready: trust is recorded as skipped for the exact boot, provisioning ends in the terminal unattested phase (no boot UUID, no path to ready), and readiness, terminal options, workspace sources and the Inception profile are refused before any host effect. Marshall refuses terminal sessions, agent turns, the agent executor binding, web search and Inception admission for such an image, hides the corresponding controls, and admits only the physical service level. An older Lilly fails closed on a firmware bundle, which is why the converged push precedes any placement. Lilly's host policy drops every unmarked packet between the host and a Customer guest, so nothing on the host reaches the Windows guest except the SSH and editor ingress paths it does not yet have. The guest bootstrap refuses an MMDS document whose fields fail validation or whose MAC is not the adapter's and never logs the token or boot challenge.

Tested scope: Protocol unit and property tests pin independent identity vectors for both manifest versions and the byte-identical version-1 bundle encoding; Lilly tests pin the kernel jail configuration and unit arguments unchanged and check the firmware variants, lifecycle edges (unattested, skipped) and firmware image loading; Marshall tests cover the capability property, admission refusal and service-level admission, and the hidden controls. The windows-lilly-guest machine test drives the real Lilly driver on nested KVM: Inception refusal before any dataset, firmware bundle on the dataset with sparse usage, skipped trust and unattested provisioning from the journals, SAC> on the boot log, the guest's bootstrap log and applied identity read from the EFI system partition of the live disk without a login, ICMP and TCP 22 on the delivered /32 from Lilly's network namespace through a test-only accept for that address in the host policy (the tap guard stays intact and its accept counter is asserted), restart from the portable bundle without the image, stop and delete. Recorded on 2026-10-14: the windows-lilly-guest test passed end to end on nested KVM (2688 s).

- **property, present**: [identity survives serialization, changes with every bound input and never collides with v4](repo:protocol/test/image_manifest_test.exs) — Version-5 manifests round-trip, change identity with firmware bytes, root digest and minima, and never share an ID with a kernel manifest; the closed eight-key shape refuses names, kernels, editors and source versions. Pure codec evidence..
- **unit, present**: [firmware bundles boot the firmware over virtio-pci with MMDS identity and no vsock](repo:lilly/test/vm/jail_test.exs) — The firmware jail configuration and MMDS metadata document, and the unchanged kernel configuration. No real jail or KVM..
- **unit, present**: [an agentless boot reaches unattested with a challenge and can never become ready](repo:lilly/test/lifecycle/provisioning_test.exs) — Lifecycle edges for the unattested phase and the skipped trust state against the durable journal; no guest..
- **unit, present**: [a firmware image admits ordinary profiles without a readiness measurement and refuses Inception](repo:marshall/test/marshall/compute/image_admission_test.exs) — Marshall registers the version-5 image, refuses Inception before allocation, admits only the physical service level and projects guest_agents_status none. Database-backed unit evidence..
- **machine, recorded**: [the host reaches the guest through a test-only policy allowance; the tap guard is intact](repo:nix/tests/machines/windows-lilly-guest/driver.py) — Real Lilly driver on nested KVM: firmware bundle, skipped trust, unattested provisioning, SAC> on the boot log, bootstrap log read from the live disk's EFI system partition, ICMP and TCP 22 on the delivered /32 through a test-only host-policy accept with the tap guard counted, restart from the bundle, delete. Passed on 2026-10-14 in 2688 s. No readiness, terminal or agent proof.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-14-windows-lilly-launch.md).

**Gaps:** Agentless launch is recorded. C: growth after a host-side extension and timekeeping under load remain unobserved; the host reachability probe is a test-only policy allowance, not a product path or Marshall observation. Agent trust/readiness, terminal, workspace, roster and executor coverage belongs to the windows-v1 scenario. Independent-host recovery and a repeatable Marshall-driven Windows matrix remain unverified.

**Preferred test work:** Add native growth/timekeeping and independent-host recovery evidence, then run the Marshall-driven multi-OS fleet journey.

## Build Arch Linux and AlmaLinux images through the existing Linux guest contract

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, execution, storage.

Boundary: Arch Linux and AlmaLinux use the version-4 kernel image shape and the reviewed foreign-Linux root assembly. Deterministic distribution markers make their catalog identities distinct; Lilly, Marshall, guest agents, trust, readiness, network and recovery remain the existing Linux path.

Required refusal: A builder missing its distribution marker, version-4 kernel/root shape or required command input is refused. This evaluation does not grant distribution-native runtime support or imply a boot.

Tested scope: The foreign-linux-guests evaluation checks Arch and Alma builder command shape, version-4 markers, names and distinct distribution identities. It is evaluation evidence only; no guest was booted.

**Gaps:** No recorded Arch or Alma Firecracker boot, guest-agent readiness, package behavior, network isolation, storage growth, relocation or recovery. The builders reuse Ubuntu assembly and do not establish distribution-native support.

**Preferred test work:** Build and run a nested-KVM Arch/Alma matrix covering boot, trust/readiness, network guard, storage growth and restart before claiming runtime support.

## Validate native FreeBSD and OmniOS guest metadata before launch

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Version-6 manifests and version-3 bundles explicitly bind FreeBSD or illumos (the OmniOS wire family) to its matching agent set, firmware boot kind, raw-GPT root and bounded artifacts. Lilly emits firmware launch metadata, attaches vsock only for protocol-speaking agents and leaves the Windows Hyper-V CPU template off native guests.

Required refusal: Unknown OS values, cross-OS agent sets, extra keys, malformed boot arguments and out-of-bound artifacts are refused. A metadata or focused jail test must not be treated as a native guest boot or fleet result.

Tested scope: Protocol validation, the native builder evaluation, local Nix realization, the focused Lilly firmware jail test and host image artifact tests cover bounded native metadata, FreeBSD CloudHv firmware configuration, preservation of the pinned FreeBSD EFI/GPT/UFS disk and manifest-declared disk.raw selection. Preliminary FreeBSD and OmniOS builders/catalog entries exist; the rolling FreeBSD 14.3 source endpoint is retired and replaced by a checksum-pinned archive capture, but no signed rollout, agent, machine boot, readiness, terminal, workspace, relocation or fleet execution exists.

- **unit, present**: [native FreeBSD firmware bundles use PVH firmware and keep the vsock transport](repo:lilly/test/vm/jail_test.exs) — Focused configuration evidence; no Firecracker process or guest.
- **unit, present**: [FreeBSD uses its complete GPT disk as the source root artifact](repo:lilly/test/host/image_test.exs) — FreeBSD selects disk.raw and illumos selects disk.raw while Lilly retains its internal rootfs.ext4 destination; this does not realize an image or boot a guest..
- **property, present**: [FreeBSD firmware shape validates for Firecracker's PVH loader](repo:protocol/test/image_manifest_test.exs) — Protocol property/unit evidence only; no image or runtime acceptance.
- **unit, present**: [native-os-guest-evaluation](repo:nix/tests/evaluation/native-os-guests.nix) — Builder source and command-shape evaluation plus local realization of the pinned preliminary artifacts; no guest is booted.

**Gaps:** No signed production image output, guest agents, nested-KVM boot, isolation, storage growth, restart/recovery or Marshall fleet evidence. The local realization is not a runtime acceptance result; OmniOS versus illumos naming remains open.

**Preferred test work:** Choose the OmniOS wire name, build signed images and add nested-KVM and Marshall-driven scenarios for boot, capability earning, isolation and recovery.

## Share a thread and use its current work machine

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Threads owns canonical viewer/operator/manager grants and immutable sharing results. Current thread grants contribute to the current matching-owner VM binding through existing Cedar authority; no VM grant is copied. Independent VM/team grants remain additive. Terminal, editor, SSH and personal guest-account authorities remain separate.

Required refusal: Refuse unregistered or retired recipients, protected-owner edits, web-only thread roles, unauthorized recipient enumeration, viewer prompts/writes, operator thread management, changed idempotency input and stale request regrant. VM-only grants do not expose task transcripts. Foreign-owner, retired or Customer bindings do not inherit thread grants. A role cannot grant another user's personal shell or bypass current login, execution or profile checks. Thread revocation removes only that authority source; independent VM/team access may remain.

Tested scope: The focused 111-case suite passed on 2026-10-16, including two properties and 109 tests. Its database-backed cases cover the role matrix, immutable replay, pending/archive/reopen continuity, scoped recipient lists, current machine visibility, additive source revocation, stale binding refusal, guest roster candidates and retired-account cleanup. The maintained browser probe passed 36 collaborator role/state/theme/viewport combinations, live viewer revoke/restore and unrelated-account refusal on synthetic fixtures. Existing production thread recovery and historical machine-sharing acceptance do not establish this new cross-domain grant path.

- **property, recorded**: [persisted roles control thread permissions and visibility](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_sharing_test.exs) — Persisted roles drive real Cedar task permissions and bounded visibility for the current actor; generated role cases use local PostgreSQL.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [delayed replay retains its receipt without restoring revoked access](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_sharing_test.exs) — A sharing request replays its immutable result after revocation without restoring the grant; changed request input refuses.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [pending and archived grants persist through reopen on the same thread](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_sharing_test.exs) — The same canonical grant remains attached to a pending, archived and reopened task; injected state does not establish physical VM replacement.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [creator is protected and lookup requires an exact existing active account](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_sharing_test.exs) — The creator cannot become a removable recipient grant; exact registered-account resolution rejects unknown or retired identities.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [bound machine authority follows current thread grants without copying them](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_sharing_test.exs) — Viewer/operator/manager grants drive current machine permissions without VM grant rows. An independent VM manager grant survives thread revocation but cannot read the task.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [viewers retain archived transcripts until their thread access is revoked](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_sharing_test.exs) — Current viewer authority permits an archived transcript and its removal refuses further task transcript reads; this uses synthetic archival.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [a retained grant follows only the current machine binding after archival and reopen](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_access_test.exs) — The retained grant follows a synthetic replacement binding, the old machine becomes unreadable through that source, and no direct VM grants are created.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [revoking a thread grant preserves independent team machine access](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_access_test.exs) — Thread manager authority is removed while independent current team membership continues granting machine write access.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [thread roster candidates require write permission and disappear after revocation](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_access_test.exs) — A roster read includes the operator's personal guest account, excludes the viewer and removes the revoked operator. No native roster delivery or live SSH revocation is exercised.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [deleted-user cleanup archives canonical thread grants and leaves shared machines intact](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_access_test.exs) — Retirement cleanup archives a recipient's thread grant with retained history and leaves a machine owned by someone else running.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [shared controls follow role changes and revocation on refresh](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/threads_live_test.exs) — The mounted page follows current sharing role and revocation on refresh; hidden controls do not replace domain refusal. Browser transport and native sessions remain separate.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [every catalogued authority-reducing action emits a hint of its kind](repo:marshall/test/marshall/control/hints_test.exs) — The authority-reduction catalog includes Thread Grant user/VM invalidations for revoke actions. This structural resource check does not prove delivery or native session closure.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **manual, recorded**: [thread-browser-role-controls](repo:marshall/scripts/monospace_browser_probe.py) — The maintained browser probe passed 36 viewer/operator/manager combinations across ready/archived/failed fixtures, both themes and two viewports, plus live fixture viewer revocation and restoration. The fixture does not run native machines or open native terminal/editor sessions.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).

**Gaps:** Native cross-node sharing races, active terminal/editor/SSH/web revocation and physical archive/reopen grant continuity remain unverified. Synthetic VM/roster and browser outcomes do not prove transport closure, guest isolation or personal-session refusal. A thread manager can intentionally delegate independent machine access, whose lifetime is separate from thread revocation.

**Preferred test work:** Add generated lock/replay/revocation histories and a NixOS create/share/use/revoke/archive/reopen journey across multiple Marshall instances. Exercise existing personal-shell refusals and surviving independent VM/team grants while terminal, editor and SSH sessions are active.

## Create and recover a user thread with its dedicated machine

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Marshall.Threads owns the durable objective, mode, team visibility and thread-to-machine binding. Compute owns VM admission/lifecycle and Agents owns turns, model calls and tools. PostgreSQL transactions, row locks, generation keys and Oban jobs coordinate durable thread changes. The authenticated LiveView is a client of these domains.

Required refusal: Reject foreign or removed-team thread access, unsupported agent profiles, invalid machine shapes, changed duplicate idempotency input, prompts before readiness, stale authority during archive/delete/reopen, mismatched transcript bindings, and prompt or terminal mutation against archived machines. The resource and database require an explicit machine admission key; malformed unkeyed provisioning and archive jobs are cancelled. A stale provision job cannot resurrect a deleting or archived thread or overwrite a new machine generation. Replacement admission waits for physical cleanup.

Tested scope: The 2026-10-16 focused 111-case suite passed the new authorization, sharing and presentation assertions, including current role controls, registered-account sharing, bounded search, newest-first transcript/top composer and editor eligibility. The maintained browser probe passed 32 thread route/theme/viewport combinations plus expanded settings/sharing views and 36 collaborator combinations. Its synthetic lifecycle evidence is separate from native acceptance. All 32 focused tests passed for the source committed in 32624e1bf80df120beb69b923eee4362427aee23, covering modes, machine shapes, titles, team authorization, idempotency, prompt replay, lifecycle actions, retained transcripts and workspace states. Five lifecycle cases use injected deletion/readiness observations; five required-key cases cover resource/database enforcement, malformed-job refusal and stale archive generation fencing. Both hosts converged healthy on that contraction, and read-only SQL confirmed NOT NULL with zero null keys. One retry on expansion revision d5e03c080204a33a11b8c136e983593630e4fccf recovered the reported thread; inspection after contraction showed the same ready thread, running VM and completed initial turn, with one VM and one turn total. Native archive/reopen, cross-node recovery and physical isolation remain unverified.

- **unit, recorded**: [index search and status filters retain only authorized matching threads](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/threads_live_test.exs) — Trimmed case-insensitive title/objective search and all/active/archived selection narrow the authorized page, survive a refresh, show an explicit no-match state and restore authorized results when cleared; another owner's matching objective remains absent.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [pending thread retains the objective while work controls remain unavailable](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/threads_live_test.exs) — The pending workspace displays its saved objective and provisioning state without prompt or terminal controls, and a forged prompt event creates no turn. This does not exercise native provisioning.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [the reply composer precedes newest-first events across refresh](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/threads_live_test.exs) — DOM order places the ready-thread composer before persisted events in descending sequence; a refresh puts a newly added event first without moving the composer below the transcript.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [editor access follows the bound machine's current eligibility](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/threads_live_test.exs) — A ready thread offers the existing machine editor route when its authorized projection is eligible; refreshing after a stop request removes the link while the thread remains ready. This is a synthetic eligibility test, not browser editor admission or guest execution.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **manual, recorded**: [thread-browser-lifecycle-coverage](repo:marshall/scripts/monospace_browser_probe.py) — The maintained browser probe passed 32 route/theme/viewport combinations, latest-first reading order, editor/terminal eligibility, list filters, four expanded machine-settings views, four expanded sharing views and unrelated-account refusal. Native provisioning and terminal/editor sessions are outside this fixture.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-16-thread-sharing.md).
- **manual, recorded**: [`machine_key` nullable `NO` and zero rows with null keys](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md) — Read-only observations on 2026-10-15 at 22:35:55-22:36:04 UTC found both hosts healthy on exact contraction revision 32624e1bf80df120beb69b923eee4362427aee23 with healthy TLS readiness. SQL confirmed NOT NULL and zero null keys. The reported recovered thread retained its same running VM and completed initial turn, with one VM and one turn total; no new retry or prompt was issued. This verifies deployment and that continuity, not native archive/reopen or fault recovery.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **manual, recorded**: [one VM existed for its machine admission key](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md) — One authorized public retry of the reported failed thread on deployed expansion revision d5e03c080204a33a11b8c136e983593630e4fccf reached ready. Read-only SQL showed one running VM for its admission key, one completed initial objective turn, and completed provisioning and title jobs. This records that recovery only; native archive/reopen, worker-loss recovery and broader provider behavior remain separate.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [thread creation requires an explicit machine admission key](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_key_test.exs) — The Ash resource refuses thread creation without its explicit machine admission key; this does not establish deployed schema convergence.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [the database refuses a missing machine admission key](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_key_test.exs) — PostgreSQL refuses an update that would remove the machine admission key after the dependent migration; this does not establish fleet deployment.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [provisioning refuses missing or malformed keys without admitting a machine](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_key_test.exs) — Missing, null, empty and non-string job keys cancel before provisioning changes thread state or admits a VM.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [archival refuses missing or malformed keys without archiving the thread](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_key_test.exs) — Missing, null, empty and non-string archive job keys cancel while the thread remains deleting.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [an archive job from an older generation cannot archive the current thread](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_machine_key_test.exs) — An archive job with a different key leaves the current generation deleting; its current key permits archival of the fixture with no bound VM.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [modes are closed and medium is the default](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_test.exs) — Closed modes, typed machine specifications and bounded title fallback are covered.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [team membership is required to create a team-scoped thread](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_test.exs) — A foreign actor is refused before team-scoped Compute admission through the coordinator authorization path.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [idempotency replay rejects a changed machine specification](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_test.exs) — A replay with the same key and changed vCPU is refused while the original thread identity remains intact.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [a ready thread routes prompts to its bound agent thread](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_test.exs) — The worker binds the registered root and thread; a prompt and request-key replay resolve to the same low-level turn.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [retry resets a failed thread and archive requires current creator](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_test.exs) — Foreign archive is refused, and the creator can retry a failed thread and archive an unbound failed thread.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [reopening an archived thread clears the old binding and queues a fresh admission](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_test.exs) — Reopen preserves the original create key, changes the machine key and clears the current binding; the fixture does not establish native VM replacement.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [create replay retains its original identity through archive and reopen](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_lifecycle_test.exs) — The original request key resolves to one high-level thread while archive and reopen change its lifecycle and machine admission key.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [archiving a pending machine retains its binding until physical deletion](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_lifecycle_test.exs) — A provisioned but pending VM keeps its thread binding while deletion is pending; an injected deleted outcome permits archival, repeated delete reuses its operation, and a later provision job cannot resurrect it.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [an existing delete intent is not proof of physical deletion](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_lifecycle_test.exs) — A VM with desired deleted state and no archived timestamp leaves the user thread deleting. This is database-state evidence, not native teardown execution.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [each reopened machine gets one initial objective and old jobs cannot change its binding](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_lifecycle_test.exs) — Three generations use distinct VMs and one objective turn each; injected running/deleted outcomes drive the lifecycle and old generation jobs leave the new binding unchanged.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [retry waits for failed machine cleanup and then rotates only the machine key](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_lifecycle_test.exs) — Retry is refused until an injected deletion outcome completes cleanup; the next admission retains the create request identity and stale jobs cannot modify the new generation.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [creator can read an archived transcript after thread archival](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_transcript_test.exs) — An archived user-thread fixture retains its authorized assistant event through the high-level transcript API.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [team member can read an archived transcript but a stranger cannot](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_transcript_test.exs) — Current team membership permits archived transcript reads while a foreign actor is refused.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [retained transcript rejects an agent thread paired with a different root](repo:marshall/test/marshall/threads_transcript_test.exs) — The high-level transcript read refuses a cross-bound agent thread instead of returning its events.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [failed provisioning exposes retry controls](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/threads_live_test.exs) — The failed workspace exposes a provisioning error and retry control through stable DOM elements.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).
- **unit, recorded**: [missing thread displays an explicit not found state and refuses mutation events](repo:marshall/test/marshall_web/threads_live_test.exs) — A missing thread renders an explicit error and prompt, archive, delete, retry and reopen events remain refused without selected state.; [execution record](repo:docs/architecture/reviews/2026-10-15-user-threads.md).

**Gaps:** No native NixOS create/prompt/archive/reopen journey, generated worker-loss/idempotency history, multi-node recovery or broader production provider acceptance is recorded. The one deployed retry and its continuity through contraction do not prove native teardown, isolation or fault recovery. Previous agent events and binding identities remain retained after reopen, but the workspace lacks a previous-generation transcript retrieval path. The terminal opens on the machine route; the requested embedded terminal and persistent global thread rail remain absent. Index search covers only the current authorized page of at most 100 threads, and activity lacks time/user/team/project filters. Synthetic browser observations do not establish cross-device runtime acceptance.

**Preferred test work:** Add an authenticated native create/prompt/archive/reopen journey, generated crash/deletion/admission histories and cross-node reconciliation. Complete embedded terminal and global thread navigation, bounded search pagination and the remaining activity filters. Add authorized previous-generation transcript retrieval and test its revocation and binding checks before claiming complete history continuity.

## Continue a long thread through automatic or requested context compaction

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: authorization, transport, execution, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Agents owns immutable context snapshots and covered-prefix checkpoints independently of retained transcript events. Existing Ziggy admission/accounting and current generation/frontier checks govern model effects and publication. Eligible-turn coverage must exclude queued later prompts and include accepted continuation exactly once.

Required refusal: Refuse oversized required instructions/current input, orphaned tool groups, stale or incomplete checkpoint results and no-progress compaction. Unknown native capability uses summary mode. A generated summary cannot change trusted mode or permissions. Ambiguous claimed model calls cannot be replayed automatically.

Tested scope: Pure compaction tests and a generated complete-tool-group property are present. No feature execution or native provider continuation result is recorded yet.

- **property, present**: [completed tool groups are either entirely summarized or entirely retained](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/compaction_test.exs) — Generated pure request groups assert that compaction does not split a completed function call from its result; no database or provider execution is included..
- **unit, present**: [only bounded completed purpose-specific output can become a checkpoint](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/compaction_test.exs) — Pure completion validation rejects malformed or incomplete summary/native output before checkpoint publication..
- **unit, present**: [unknown native support uses tool-free summaries and keeps instructions outside generated state](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/compaction_test.exs) — Pure request construction selects the summary fallback without verified native support and preserves separate trusted instructions..

**Gaps:** SQL checkpoint publication races, queued-prompt interleaving, restart continuation, real Ziggy/provider/guest composition and native provider capability acceptance remain unverified. Pure input/output validation does not establish durable replay or transport effects.

**Preferred test work:** Execute generated SQL checkpoint/replay histories and a NixOS Marshall-to-Ziggy-to-provider-fixture-to-guest continuation including claim loss, stale completion and repeated compaction; verify live native compaction separately before enabling it.

## Plan work, answer durable questions and implement the exact completed proposal

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, execution, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Persisted Plan/Work collaboration is trusted application state separate from reasoning. Root Plan turns own durable questions and the first current authorized answer. A completed proposal binds one Work turn on the same thread and machine. Plan is instructional and does not create a read-only filesystem boundary.

Required refusal: Viewers and revoked actors cannot answer or implement. Child agents cannot ask human questions. Stale, cancelled, archived or nonactive requests refuse. Competing answers cannot overwrite a winner, changed replay inputs conflict and partial proposal output cannot be implemented. Mode changes while work runs or awaits answers refuse.

Tested scope: Pure proposal/question tests, a generated truncated-proposal property and SQL-backed persistence cases are present. Their execution and browser/native behavior are not yet recorded.

- **property, present**: [a prefix cut before the closing tag never grants an implementation identity](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/planning_test.exs) — Generated incomplete proposal prefixes cannot acquire a valid implementation identity in the pure parser..
- **unit, present**: [the first answer wins, retries are stable, and the tool result is appended once](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/planning_persistence_test.exs) — SQL-backed sequential answer and replay cases inspect one accepted answer and one durable tool result. This is not a simultaneous multi-node race..
- **unit, present**: [current write authority is required, including on an otherwise valid replay](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/planning_persistence_test.exs) — SQL-backed current-authority checks refuse answering after write authority is lost, including immutable request replay..
- **unit, present**: [only completed plans are retained and implementation binds one exact Work turn](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/planning_persistence_test.exs) — Persisted proposal identity and implementation callback bind the completed proposal to one Work turn; this fixture does not run the provider or guest..

**Gaps:** Concurrent answer winner schedules, cross-node restart, archive/live revocation, complete provider/tool continuation and browser keyboard/mobile question/proposal flows remain unverified. The instructional mode does not enforce operating-system read-only execution.

**Preferred test work:** Run the SQL persistence suite and generated question/revocation histories, then a NixOS browser and guest journey that restarts Marshall while awaiting input, races answers and implements only the accepted exact proposal.

## Apply the thread creator's guest and project guidance with explicitly selected skills

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Current fenced guest operations resolve the creator's durable guest home and collect machine, creator and repository guidance. Verified source digests and complete selected skill bodies enter immutable snapshots; Marshall host files and a collaborator's personal configuration are not sources. Skill content remains subordinate to application mode, ACL and profile policy.

Required refusal: Refuse unsafe linked instruction paths, stale digests, malformed chunks, nonexistent creator profiles, stale execution and required content beyond context limits. Skip linked skill directories with a warning. Duplicate skill names require explicit identity; unsupported connectors do not install automatically.

Tested scope: Rust local-file tests for precedence, worktrees, chunk verification and linked paths are present. No execution or native Linux/Windows authenticated discovery is yet recorded.

- **unit, present**: [precedence_empty_override_and_scope_are_explicit](repo:lyra/src/agent/context.rs) — Local temporary-directory fixtures assert machine/creator/project order and different empty override semantics..
- **unit, present**: [chunks_preserve_bytes_and_refuse_changed_files](repo:lyra/src/agent/context.rs) — Local file reads preserve byte chunks and reject changed content under an earlier digest..
- **unit, present**: [linked_files_and_cycles_are_not_followed](repo:lyra/src/agent/context.rs) — Local filesystem fixtures reject linked instruction files and avoid cycling through linked skill directories..

**Gaps:** Native Windows SID/profile lookup, Linux account resolution through authenticated operations, changed-file retry histories, complete skill selection across compaction and transport revocation remain unverified. Local temporary-file tests do not establish VM or host filesystem isolation.

**Preferred test work:** Execute generated file/chunk/parser histories and Linux/Windows native guest discovery through the full fenced control path, including changed content, oversized skills, duplicate identities and current-authority loss.

## Install and manage a pinned public skill, then restore it when reopening a thread

Coverage: **partial**. Layers: identity, authorization, transport, execution, isolation, storage, recovery.

Boundary: Current thread managers configure managed installations; operators invoke enabled versions. Ash resources own immutable request identity and pinned revision metadata, while fenced guest operations fetch and materialize immutable revision directories outside repository discovery roots.

Required refusal: Refuse nonmanager mutations, stale execution, private credentials, unapproved repository hosts, traversal, changed replay input and unexpected digest. Fetching runs no hooks, filters, submodules or installation scripts. Failed updates retain the active revision; managed removal cannot erase personal or project skill files or locally edited content.

Tested scope: SQL manager/replay/recovery tests and native-library local Git/filesystem tests are present. No managed installation execution is recorded here yet.

- **unit, present**: [manager revocation before publication refuses a successful guest result](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/skills_test.exs) — SQL fixture publication rechecks management authority before accepting a synthetic successful guest descriptor..
- **unit, present**: [ambiguous results reconcile from status without repeating installation](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/skills_test.exs) — SQL operation state resolves a synthetic status response without invoking a second install dispatch..
- **unit, present**: [replacement machine restoration pins the prior commit and digest](repo:marshall/test/marshall/agents/skills_test.exs) — A synthetic changed machine generation restores recorded revision identity rather than following a branch..
- **unit, present**: [extracts_real_blobs_without_running_attributes_filters_or_hooks](repo:lyra/src/agent/managed_skills.rs) — Local real-Git fixtures exercise ordinary blob extraction without checkout hooks or configured filters; no guest transport is involved..
- **unit, present**: [status_reports_missing_and_preserves_edited_revisions](repo:lyra/src/agent/managed_skills.rs) — Local receipt/status inspection distinguishes missing operation state from installed content changed after publication..

**Gaps:** Manager/role histories, URL/path attacks, interrupted installation reconciliation, local-edit preservation, immutable revision retention and pinned physical reopen restoration remain unverified on Linux and Windows.

**Preferred test work:** Add and execute generated source/operation histories plus native Linux/Windows install/update/remove/reopen journeys using public-repository fixtures, exact digest assertions, mid-operation guest loss and current-authority withdrawal.
